    F-35A Lightning II — First Flight
kind: documentary
style: Live-action widescreen documentary realism; steady tracking views, natural daylight, and AA-1’s documented proportions, geometry and markings.
In the opening two seconds, a widescreen live-action view shows AA-1, the first F-35A Lightning II prototype, lifting off at Lockheed Martin in Fort Worth, Texas, at 12:44 p.m. Central Standard Time on December 15, 2006. The title reads “F-35A Lightning II.” Chief pilot Jon Beesley remains an indistinct silhouette beneath the dark canopy.

From seconds five through eight, the view follows AA-1 flying after departure. “Conventional takeoff” replaces the previous fact label, while the narrator says, “It takes off from a conventional runway.” These successive views show an excerpt of the inaugural development test flight, not its full thirty-five-minute duration.

Maya argues for government spending in a recession, and Leo argues that spending and cheap money generate the boom that leads to a bust. Maya tilts her coffee mug toward Leo and jokes about another coffee; Leo lifts his mug toward hers, smiling, while insisting that the economy should correct itself.

  1. 1. story 0 cr
    research: Citric acid cycle — research for one complete turn inside a mitochondrion, rather than a historical discovery episode. Dates below identify source publication dates, not dates when these recurring biochemical reactions occurred. The 60-second running time is a presentation choice, not the mea … (427 chars)
    text (1244 chars)
    Citric acid cycle
kind: explainer
style: 60-second widescreen explainer in simple, clean 2D animation using textbook-style diagrams; calm English narration; no music.
Under “Citric acid cycle,” an oval mitochondrial cutaway shows a symbolic, arrow-linked loop in the matrix. Labels identify molecules as calm English narration follows the reactions. Acetyl-CoA supplies a two-carbon acetyl group to four-carbon oxaloacetate, forming six-carbon citrate; CoA-SH is released.

Citrate rearranges into six-carbon isocitrate. Isocitrate becomes five-carbon α-ketoglutarate: CO₂ leaves and the first NADH appears. Next, four-carbon succinyl-CoA forms, releasing a second CO₂ and producing a second NADH. The departing carbons are not the newly arrived acetyl carbons.

Succinyl-CoA becomes succinate, releasing CoA-SH; a GTP → ATP callout represents one ATP equivalent, not two additional products. Membrane-embedded succinate dehydrogenase converts succinate to fumarate as FAD becomes FADH₂. Water enters as fumarate becomes malate.

Malate becomes oxaloacetate, producing the third NADH. The regenerated oxaloacetate can accept another acetyl group, closing the loop. The completed turn shows two CO₂, three NADH, one FADH₂ and one ATP equivalent.

    CITRIC ACID CYCLE — ONE COMPLETE TURN  (60 s; 10 scenes, 10 shots)
synopsis: Within a mitochondrial cutaway, labeled molecules follow a clockwise citric acid cycle as calm English narration explains the reactions. Acetyl-CoA contributes two carbons to oxaloacetate, and successive reactions release two CO₂ while producing reducing equivalents and one ATP equivalent. The incoming acetyl carbons remain distinct from the carbons released during this turn, and the membrane-associated succinate dehydrogenase step produces FADH₂. Water enters, malate regenerates oxaloacetate, and the completed cycle holds its totals: two CO₂, three NADH, one FADH₂, and one ATP equivalent.
theme: Renewal preserves continuity while releasing usable energy.
music: Instrumental minimalist ambient score, calm and precise, soft marimba, warm sustained synthesizer and muted pulse, 80 bpm, unobtrusive beneath English narration.   arc: [0s] Begin a restrained, even pulse beneath the narration.  [30s] Add a soft harmonic lift without increasing tempo.  [54s] Resolve gently and sustain through the final accounting; end at 60 seconds.

The video presents a mitochondrial cycle diagram with English narration, but inaccurate explanations and garbled labels make it unsuitable for first-year teaching without a redo.
The narration contains misleading biology: it associates FADH2 production with fumarate becoming FADH2 and says oxaloacetate yields another carbon dioxide group. These confuse the reactions and the regeneration step. · Several diagram annotations are garbled or incomplete, including 'ANADH', an unreadable enzyme annotation near fumarate, and leader lines without labels. Key intermediates and products are not consistently identifiable. · The illustration uses heavily shaded, textured, three-dimensional-looking organelles and molecular blobs rather than the requested simple, clean 2D animation.
